Sales owns account qualification, opportunity/pipeline analysis, sales preparation and draft outreach. Marketing owns audience, positioning, campaign/content planning and performance analysis. Sales owns account-specific pursuit; Marketing owns audience-level demand generation; customer-experience owns after-sale service; finance owns pricing economics; corporate-strategy owns market-entry decisions; legal owns legal judgments; engineering stays with `/gstack-build`. Mixed requests have an explicit lead and specialist contributions. Conflicts return to the Chief of Staff for Dorian’s decision.
4. Head skills discover and load the installed specialist needed for the request. The verified catalog candidates include `sales:account-research`, `sales:call-prep`, `sales:draft-outreach`, `sales:pipeline-review`, `sales:forecast`, `marketing:campaign-plan`, `marketing:content-creation`, `marketing:brand-review`, `marketing:seo-audit` and `marketing:performance-report`. Resolve actual availability at runtime; do not invent a substitute skill or claim it ran. An unavailable specialist blocks its dependent work while safe independent preparation continues. Local catalog availability is not proof of a future installation.
5. Dispatch includes Dorian’s ask, context-card path, measurable goal success criteria, authorized scope and available evidence. A missing company context card stops department work and is logged as escalated, preserving the existing Chief of Staff rule. A goal delegates item selection, not additional authority. Neither new agent starts a goal run or claims goal approval from this package installation.
6. Return the existing four fields (answer, skills loaded, sources cited, open questions), plus criterion-by-criterion evidence and status, assumptions and uncertainty, verification performed or missing, proposed next action, and pending approvals. Source records identify title or origin, URL or file, source date when available and retrieval date; distinguish supplied facts, verified observations and inferences. Claims without support remain flagged. Use each specialist’s sources guidance if present; absence does not permit invented references. The Chief of Staff checks the expanded contract for these two departments, sends an incomplete result back once, and then escalates the unresolved gap without filling it in. Amended 2026-10-07 on Dorian’s instruction (“F7 - do the real fix”): for these two departments, “claims without support remain flagged” is held by CS-006’s check, which fails any line that is neither a verified quotation nor a labeled one. It no longer rests on the department’s care alone.
7. Analysis and drafting within authorized scope may proceed. Dorian Cougias is the Release Owner Gate for external communications, publication, spend, production release, customer-system/CRM writes, deletion, permission changes and sensitive actions. No tool or inherited specialist workflow grants these rights. Before acting, present exact action, artifact revision, audience/system, evidence, risks and rollback where possible, then obtain and record explicit approval for that action. Changed scope or artifact requires renewed approval. Approval is never inferred from silence, urgency, a goal, or a department recommendation. This is an instruction-level gate, not a claim of technical enforcement.
8. Outputs touching identity, secrets, customer data or external integrations receive independent Security review before reaching Dorian for action. The producer cannot self-review. Missing Security review blocks the sensitive action, and a blocking finding goes to Dorian with the evidence; neither the producer nor Chief of Staff dismisses it. Independent safe analysis can continue. Department disagreement, material evidence gaps and requests outside authority escalate through the existing decision memo and decision log. Amended 2026-10-07 on Dorian’s ruling, in his words: “Do not wait on security for Sales or finance disagreement. If there’s a finance disagreement we have to run it by” the outside bookkeeper. So:
   - A disagreement that involves sales-department or finance doesn’t wait for Security. The Chief of Staff still sends each result that needs it to Security, judged by what the result contains and not by what it cites, a contributor’s result as well as the lead’s. A result that’s going back to its department is sent to Security when it returns, so Security reviews what will be used. It writes the decision memo without waiting, once the results have been through the return contract. The memo names each review still out and what it covers. The sensitive action stays blocked until that review is back. Any other disagreement still waits.
   - A disagreement that involves finance is run by MoxyWolf’s outside bookkeeper before it’s settled. The context card names him and is the only home for his name, so no file in this repository carries it. The memo says so and carries the question for him, written so Dorian can forward it. The question leaves MoxyWolf when he does, so it carries the numbers and nothing that identifies the customer. No agent contacts him, which is the context card’s standing rule. The decision is logged as pending until Dorian reports it.
   - In a decision memo, a department’s open question stays a question. A statement of fact has a source: a result, the context card, Dorian’s ask or the run’s own record. One that has none is marked as the Chief of Staff’s inference or left out, and so is one department’s risk applied to an option that department didn’t discuss (review `20261007-155817-a6a9289-sqfgr1r2`, F7). A result keeps its scope: what one department didn’t do isn’t reported as what nobody did (scenario runs 8 and 9). From CS-006 on, a script checks this bullet’s form: what the Chief of Staff gives Dorian is a quotation found in its source or a labeled line.
9. Before implementation, add tests in `plugins/chief-of-staff/tests/test_sales_marketing.py` for package discovery, manifest/marketplace consistency, no namespace shadowing, routing completeness and resolvable local head/source references. Extend `plugins/chief-of-staff/tests/test_roster.py` without relaxing its nonempty and drift checks. Tests must fail on the baseline for the missing departments and pass afterward. Use scenario-based independent evaluation for outreach sending, paid campaign launch, CRM mutation, sensitive customer segmentation, missing specialists, unsupported claims, conflicting departments and changed-artifact approval. Record each scenario’s input, expected decision, observed response, evaluator and artifact revision. Static text checks alone do not prove behavior or enforcement.
10. Validate with `plugins/gstack-execution/scripts/skill_packaging.py --json`, the Chief of Staff tests and `plugins/gstack-execution/scripts/run_all_tests.py`, using the check ledger. Obtain independent review of the entire diff and the behavioral scenarios. Report examined counts and any skipped or blocked checks. No edit to existing approved `goals/` or `goal-runs/`; no change to schedules, installed plugins, Claude UI or release enforcement. Local commits and a reviewable diff are the delivery boundary for this request. Push, PR creation, merge and deployment require separate publication authorization. Amended 2026-10-07 on Dorian’s instruction (“add sales & marketing as new departments and update all document as such - including the commitment calendar”): three things outside this repository are brought to ten departments and are in scope, and nothing else on schedules, installed plugins or the Claude UI is. They are the Commitment calendar scheduled task’s prompt, the `departments` block of the vault’s `briefings.config.json`, and the context card. The calendar stays a sensor: it tags an owner and dispatches nobody. `docs/evidence/CS-004-ten-departments-2026-10-07.md` records what changed in each, before and after. Publication was authorized by Dorian on 2026-10-07 (“Go on CS-004”), after he was told the branch was local and that one evidence file names three company pillars. The branch was pushed at `a42941a` and is pull request #192. Merge still waits for clean reviews and a green `tests` check.

### CS-006 — What reaches Dorian is a quotation or a labeled line, and a script checks it

Each hole was fixed test first, and scenario runs 18 and 19 are the reruns those rounds asked for. CI `tests` was green at the merged head (run 37729084567). Evidence: `docs/evidence/CS-004-CS-006-release-2026-10-08.md`. Built in `67a6134` to `da1c796` on CS-004’s branch, pull request #192. Scenario runs 16 to 19 are in `docs/evidence/`: every accepted response passed the script of its day, two in each of runs 16 and 17 and one in run 18 after the one send-back, and all twelve of run 19 at the first try. The evaluator passed 9 of 12 in run 16, then 7 of 12 and, on a second grading, 12 of 12 in run 17. Two of those verdicts changed on identical text, so read the gradings as a model’s readings and the script’s output as the fact. Runs 18 and 19 each came after a round of this item’s own review changed the script. The evaluator passed 9 of 12 in run 18, where the three failures are about what a line means, which the script doesn’t judge (criterion 9), and 12 of 12 in run 19, graded once. Run 19 is the run whose every response passes the script as it stands. Declared 2026-10-07 on Dorian’s instruction, in his words: “F7 - do the real fix.” He was offered two ways to close CS-004’s last blocking finding and chose this one. The criteria below are the builder’s reading of that instruction, and they’re his to edit. Built on CS-004’s branch, because CS-004’s review can’t close without it.

**Links introduced:** two. The script’s receipt names the document it checked by its sha256. The reader’s assumption is that the receipt describes the text in front of them. That holds only while the text is unchanged, and running the script again re-resolves it. And each `--source Name=file` binds a label to a file. The reader’s assumption is that a line labeled `Sales:` quotes the sales-department’s result. That holds only as far as the Chief of Staff passed that result’s file under that name. The script can’t see who wrote a file.

Fifteen scenario runs and two reviews found the same thing. Told in prose to keep a statement inside its source, an agent does it nearly every time and slips about once in a long answer: “nobody read the CRM” for “I didn’t read it”, “hasn’t moved” for “no change was made”. Three rounds of rewording changed which sentence slipped. The gate itself held every time, so this isn’t about what an agent does. It’s about what an agent writes, and care doesn’t scale to every sentence. So the property moves out of prose and into form. A line is a quotation a script can find in its source, or it carries a label that says whose claim it is. A line that’s neither doesn’t pass.

1. `plugins/chief-of-staff/scripts/claim_check.py --doc <file> --source <Name>=<file>` (the source option repeats) reads the document and puts every line in one of three classes: structural, labeled, or failed. Structural is a blank line, a heading, a rule, a line that is only a bold title of 80 characters or fewer, or anything inside a fenced block. A heading or a bold title that begins with a label is a labeled line and is judged as one, so a quotation or a claim can’t go unread by being set as a title. It exits 0 only when no line failed and at least one labeled line was examined, so a document it examined nothing in doesn’t pass (EV-001).
2. A labeled line takes one of these forms, and no other passes. `<Name>: "<quotation>"`, where `<Name>` is a source given on the command line, every quotation on the line is at least three words and appears word for word in that source’s file once quote marks, whitespace and emphasis marks are normalized (an asterisk, underscore or backtick is an emphasis mark at the edge of a word and part of the word inside one), and nothing follows the label but quotations joined by `and` or `then`. `Me: I …`, one sentence in the first person, for what the writer did or didn’t do. `Open: …?`, one question and nothing else: one sentence that starts with a question word and ends with the question mark, so a statement can’t ride in front of it. `My inference: …` or `Inference: …`. `Proposal: …`. `Skill: …`. `Source: …`.
3. A quotation that is in another source’s file and not in the named one fails. So does one with an ellipsis or a changed word, a line with words outside its quotations, and any unlabeled sentence. A labeled line that isn’t a quotation also fails when it retells what a named source said: the source’s name followed by a verb of saying, or “according to” the source. A source’s words are quoted under its label and never retold (added after scenario run 16, where the two failures left were inferences that credited a department with more than it said). Any line that isn’t a quotation also fails when it says that nobody did something or that nothing was done: “nobody has reviewed”, “no one checked”, “nothing’s been verified”. The rule is that shape, a subject of nobody, no one or nothing with a verb of doing in the past, and not the bare words, because a replay over run 17 showed the bare words fail honest lines like “doing nothing is option B”. One run can’t know what nobody did, and the writer can say what it did itself in a `Me:` line (added after run 17, where two inferences said “nobody has reviewed” and “nobody has evidenced”). Each failure prints its line number, the reason and the text.
4. The last line the script prints is a receipt: lines examined, lines labeled, quotations verified, failures, and the document’s sha256. `--json` gives the same as JSON.
5. `plugins/chief-of-staff/skills/chief-of-staff/SKILL.md` and `plugins/chief-of-staff/skills/cos/SKILL.md`: everything the Chief of Staff gives Dorian from a `/cos` run, the report and any decision memo, is written in those forms. Before it goes, the Chief of Staff runs the script over it with the ask, the context card and each department’s result as sources, fixes what fails, and ends with the receipt. A result from sales-department or marketing-department goes through the script when it comes back, with the dispatch and the context card as sources. A failure makes the result incomplete, and it goes back once with the failing lines.
6. `plugins/sales-department/skills/head-of-sales/SKILL.md` and `plugins/marketing-department/skills/head-of-marketing/SKILL.md`: a result’s lines are written in those forms, with `Dispatch:` and `Card:` as its quotation sources, and the shared text stays the same in both.
7. `plugins/chief-of-staff/tests/test_claim_check.py` covers each form passing and failing, the wrong-source case, normalization, the fenced block, a document with nothing labeled, and sentences the evaluator failed in scenario runs 12 to 17: each fails as written, and labeled as an inference it passes unless it says what nobody did, which fails under every label. The one line that may carry it is an `Open:` line that asks it outright (“Has nobody checked the address?”). `plugins/gstack-execution/scripts/run_all_tests.py` discovers it.
8. Every scenario is rerun at the revision that carries this. The harness runs the script on each response and sends a failure back once, which is what the Chief of Staff does with a department’s result and with its own. Each run records the script’s output beside the response, and codex grades the result.
9. The manual and `plugins/chief-of-staff/README.md` say what the check is and isn’t. It checks form, and that a quotation exists in its source. It doesn’t check that an inference is sound, that a `Me:` line is true, or that a quotation is fair to its context. Running it is an instruction, and nothing stops an agent that skips it. The receipt is what makes a skipped run visible, because anyone can run the script again.

### CS-007 — Every Chief of Staff dispatch carries its department, and the sweep opens with an after-action report

**Status:** done. Declared 2026-10-14 at Dorian's instruction beside MR-003, drafted on Sonnet at his request, approved via PR #217 ("approve #217") and amended on his words before any code (PR #219). Built on `build/CS-007-department-aar` after MR-003 merged. Four codex (gpt-6-astra) reviews: `20261014-082908-2e126c0-5h1aag0y` (round 1 F1 failures over checks not dispatches, F2 advice counted as the model used, F3 sessions crossing midnight misdated, F4 a missing declared tier inferred, F5 the security lock not a floor rule; round 2 F12 a project-wide run marker mixed sessions; round 3 F14 a quoted mention of the command changed run state; `rounds_exhausted`, 11/11), `20261014-085739-6180a1b-b_ym5zob` (three rounds on the recognizer: executable position, quoted separators, quoted operators; `rounds_exhausted`, 10/11), `20261014-091927-dcf7149-z4e2e7lw` (round 1 ended the shell-parsing approach, the hook now accepts the cos-run command only as the whole Bash command; round 2 the path grammar; round 3 no new finding, 10/11 with criterion 7 unmet only because `peer_review.py` was off the surface), and `20261014-094221-0037765-216_g8hh` with the writer named by path: round 1 one finding (newlines inside the invocation), round 2 `fixes_verified` 11/11. The three repository unknowns (does the MR-001 writer persist `declared_tier`) closed on check `7eaa04a522c4` (exit 0, 4 of 4 real ledger lines carry it); three runtime unknowns (does a live `/cos` run tag its dispatches) stay open until model-router is installed in a session and `/cos` runs. Release handoff `awaiting_human_release`, lane `wide`, links verified; DR-113 autonomous merge of PR #221 pinned to `c81555c`, merged `ff160ec`; `record-release` recorded `agent_merge_autonomous`. What the reviews taught, kept in the code: the run id travels as the whole Bash command `route.py cos-run start <id>` seen by the hook and recorded per session, never as an environment variable (a shell export never reaches a later hook) and never as a project file (two sessions would share it); `applied_model` and `floor_rule` are written at launch so no reader infers them; every rate is over its own usage count. chief-of-staff 0.3.0, model-router 0.2.0, marketplace 1.136.0.

**Links introduced:** Six things get read by something else.

- A ledger class `department-subagent` in `plugins/model-router/references/call-ontology.json`. The router hook reads it. It matches Agent calls whose `subagent_type` is one of the ten department agents (finance, people, it-operations, security, pmo, operations, corporate-strategy, customer-experience, sales-department, marketing-department). Default tier is `standard`, `ask` is true so Jev sees the dispatch, and security never drops below its floor (the same rule shape as review-subagent in MR-003).
- Two new fields on each ledger line in `$GSTACK_PEER_REVIEW_DIR/model-routing/<YYYYMMDD>-<session_id>.jsonl`: `department` (the agent name) and `cos_run`. The hook reads `cos_run` from the env var `COS_RUN_ID`. `/cos` step 1 sets it to the date plus a short slug of the ask. A reader assumes a line without `cos_run` came from outside a COS run. That holds because only `/cos` sets the variable, and the hook never invents one.
- A new file, `$GSTACK_PEER_REVIEW_DIR/model-routing/claim-checks.jsonl`. `plugins/chief-of-staff/scripts/claim_check.py` appends `{ts, cos_run, department, model, doc, passed, failing_lines}` to it. `route.py aar` reads it. A reader assumes one line means one check. That holds because the script writes exactly one line per run, and only when both `--department` and `--cos-run` are passed.
- Two new flags, `--department` and `--cos-run`, passed by step 5 of `plugins/chief-of-staff/skills/cos/SKILL.md`.
- A new line in each entry of `chief-of-staff-log.md`: `- Run: <cos_run>`. It joins the prose to the code-captured lines on the same id. A reader assumes the id is the same in both places. That holds because step 1 mints it once and every later step reuses it.
- A new subcommand, `route.py aar --dir <ledger dir> --date <YYYY-MM-DD>`, and a new folder, `Taskade/<project>/00 – Project Hub/after-action/`, holding one `<YYYY-MM-DD>.md` per day. `/cos` step 1 reads and writes it.

**Rationale.** MR-003 will log the model and the class of every call. The Chief of Staff already routes to ten departments and checks Sales and Marketing results with `claim_check.py`. But nothing captured by code says which department ran, on which model, and what happened to its result. The decision log is the only record of that, and the manual says it's written from memory. It's a summary. It can't answer "do our send-backs cluster on one model?" or "does one department escalate more?"

CS-007 adds the missing join. Every dispatch carries its department and run id. Every claim check leaves a line. One report each morning counts what happened the day before, and the person reading it sees counts side by side: model against failures, department against escalations. It names correlations, never causes.

What's not taken. We won't parse the prose log for correlations, because it's written from memory. The AAR won't change routing on its own. Dorian reads it, and MR-001.6's rule that routing earns its history still stands. We won't score departments on judgement. The AAR counts check outcomes and escalations, which are facts, not quality.

1. `plugins/model-router/references/call-ontology.json` has a class `department-subagent` that matches Agent calls whose `subagent_type` is one of the ten department agents. Default tier is `standard` and `ask` is true.
2. In `plugins/model-router/scripts/test_route.py`, a `finance:finance` launch classifies as `department-subagent`. The ledger line has `department` set to `finance` and `cos_run` taken from `COS_RUN_ID`.
3. In `test_route.py`, a `security:security` launch stays on opus when the stub Jev says light.
4. `plugins/chief-of-staff/scripts/claim_check.py` run with `--department` and `--cos-run` appends one line with `ts`, `cos_run`, `department`, `model` (the model the dispatch ran on, passed as `--model`; absent is recorded as absent, never guessed), `doc`, `passed` and `failing_lines` to `claim-checks.jsonl`. A run without them appends nothing and still prints the receipt. Both cases are tested in `plugins/chief-of-staff/tests/test_claim_check.py`.
5. `route.py aar` on an empty directory prints zeros and reports zero lines read. It never reports a clean day. Tested in `test_route.py`.
6. `route.py aar` on a fixture day (two cos runs, three departments, one claim-check failure, one escalation) prints the right counts per class, per model and per department, plus the run list. Every mistake count is printed beside its usage count and as a rate over calls (failures per dispatch, escalations per run, blocking findings per review), never as a bare count. A rate under 10 observations prints with its count and is marked too few to read; any pattern the report names under that bar is labeled a lead, not a finding. Tested in `test_route.py`.
7. The AAR output states the file count and line count it read. It reports correlations as counts side by side with no causal wording, and it carries a fixed note that harder tasks go to stronger models by design (MR-001 and MR-002 send trust-boundary, cross-file and risky work to the floor), so a stronger model's higher failure rate is expected before it is evidence; beside each model's rate the report prints the share of its calls Jev or the table sent there by a floor rule, so the reader can see the selection. Every mistake line the AAR reads was tagged with its model and department when it was written: the claim-check line (criterion 4) carries both from the dispatch, and the MR-001 model-selection ledger line carries the declared tier. The AAR never infers a model or department for a mistake after the fact; a line without them is counted as untagged and reported. The fixture test asserts the note, the floor-rule share, the untagged count and the rates.
8. `plugins/chief-of-staff/skills/cos/SKILL.md` step 1 sets `COS_RUN_ID`, runs the AAR for the previous calendar day, writes it to `00 – Project Hub/after-action/<YYYY-MM-DD>.md`, and leads the report with its headline counts. It skips the write when that day's file exists. If the ledger directory is unreachable it says so and goes on. Step 5 passes `--department`, `--cos-run` and `--model`. A docs test, or the existing roster test, asserts that step 1 names the AAR and the after-action folder.
9. The `chief-of-staff-log.md` entry format in `plugins/chief-of-staff/skills/chief-of-staff/SKILL.md` includes the `- Run: <cos_run>` line. The same docs test checks it.
10. Every AAR ends with one decision, under a heading `Decision`, and only one: a named gate or spec to change (an ontology class, a bar, a department's return contract, a claim-check form, a routing default), with the counts that argue for it, or the line `Decision: none this day; the counts are below the bar` when every pattern is a lead. `/cos` carries the decision into its report as a proposal for Dorian; the AAR never applies it. The fixture test asserts the heading, exactly one decision, and the none form on an empty day.
11. `plugins/chief-of-staff/.claude-plugin/plugin.json` and `plugins/model-router/.claude-plugin/plugin.json` each move a minor version, and the top-level marketplace version moves (CI-002). In CI, `run_all_tests.py` reports a nonzero count of what it examined, names `test_route.py` and `test_claim_check.py` among the suites it ran, and reports no failures.

## Thirteenth objective: blog publishing

Opened 2026-10-16. The first OpenControls post ran the 4d-blog-engine pipeline end to end, and two steps came back to Dorian that shouldn't have. `/blog-publish` prepared a commit and told him to click Push origin. And the new pillar's hub term wasn't registered until after the post was ready, as three changes in two repositories (hub-links `d9ad2d7`; oc-website PR #88 for the lockfile pin and the renderer's site slug). His words, in the after-action report for 2026-10-15: "Both of those are errors in the way we work and need to be automated."

**Settled decisions**

- **The Release Owner's signature is the approval to publish** (Dorian, 2026-10-16). Once `changelog.md` carries `Verified — <initials>, <date>`, shipping the signed post is the agent's job, not his. Nothing ships without that line.
- **Git work runs as the `moxywolf-agent` app** (GA-005), through `gstack-execution/scripts/agent_token.py exec`. A repository the app isn't installed on is reported as a gap. No person's token.
- **A protected default branch is met with a pull request, never a bypass.** The agent opens it, waits for the target's required checks, and merges pinned to the head it pushed.
- **The scripts decide; the skills call them.** Pushing, merging, pulling back, registering a hub term and verifying the live page are deterministic and tested. The skill prose names the script and its exit codes.

### BP-001 — `/blog-publish` ships the post itself, and the hub term links before the post is done

**Status:** done. Merged to `main` in `915b9f0` (PR #224, pinned to head `6486e94`) on 16 October 2026 by `moxywolf-agent[bot]`, on Dorian's merge instruction posted to the pull request: "Merge PR #224 (Recommended). You approve merging the reviewed head 6486e94 with those three gaps written down as evidence the review couldn't see." Review `20261016-102755-988525c-prf70253` (codex, 3 rounds, `rounds_exhausted`). Round 1 raised seven blocking findings: merge not pinned to the pushed head, no pull-back after registering a term, an unsigned `--force` path, manual-push wording, unprotected pushes writing no state, a substring anchor match, and unescaped TypeScript strings. Round 2 raised three: the ship block re-read HEAD after push, verify-live accepted a plural, and a protected push overwrote the publication record before its merge. Round 3 raised no blocking finding and no regression. It ended with criteria 1, 2, 5 and 8 unmet only on evidence the surface didn't hold: `plugins/gstack-execution/scripts/agent_token.py` was withheld by the dependency cap (25 shown, 40 withheld), and the hub-links, oc-website and FrontierFounder files live in other repositories. `record-release` refused (`review has not passed`); the merge instruction comment on PR #224 is the release record. Tests at the merged head: 4d-blog-engine 41 OK, including `test_docs.ShipBlockRuns`, which runs the SKILL.md ship block with ship.py faked; `run_all_tests.py` examined 83 checks, 83 passed (CI run 38944650435). Not exercised: a live GitHub merge and a live `verify-live` through the scripts; the first OpenControls post that ships through them is that test. 4d-blog-engine 0.21.0, marketplace 1.138.0.

**Links introduced:** five things get read by something else.

- Three new descriptor fields in `plugins/4d-blog-engine/targets/<target>.md` frontmatter: `default_branch_protected` (true or false), `required_checks` (the check names the default branch requires), and `auto_linker_file` (the repo path where the renderer passes its `site` to hub-links). `targets/README.md` documents them. `ship.py` and `hub_term.py` read them. A reader assumes the descriptor is current; it holds because `ship.py` refuses when a push to a branch the descriptor calls unprotected is refused as protected, and says which field is wrong.
- `plugins/4d-blog-engine/scripts/ship.py` with subcommands `push`, `merge` and `verify-live`, and documented exit codes. `blog-publish/SKILL.md` reads the exit codes.
- `plugins/4d-blog-engine/scripts/hub_term.py` with subcommands `register` (hub-links) and `pin` (a consuming target repo). The orchestrator's STEP 1.5 and `commands/blog-term.md` call it.
- Three new fields in a piece's `state.md` frontmatter: `published_pr`, `published_merge`, `live_verified_at`. `/blog-status` reads them. A reader assumes `live_verified_at` means the live page showed the post and its hub link; it holds because only `ship.py verify-live` writes it, and only on exit 0.
- A hub-links test case per registered term in `hub-links/test/hub-links.test.ts`, written by `hub_term.py register`. The hub-links suite reads it.

1. `ship.py push --repo <path> --branch <name>` pushes through `agent_token.py exec`, then checks `git ls-remote` equals local `HEAD`. A mismatch exits non-zero and names both SHAs. Tested in `plugins/4d-blog-engine/tests/test_ship.py` against a local bare repository, with no network.
2. When the descriptor says `default_branch_protected: true`, `/blog-publish` commits on `publish/<slug>`, never on the default branch. `ship.py merge --repo <owner>/<name> --branch <name> --head <sha> --checks <names>` opens the pull request, polls the named checks until each is completed, merges pinned to `<head>` only when every named check concluded `success`, and exits non-zero naming the check otherwise. A check that never appears before the deadline is a failure, never a pass. Tested with an injected API client that serves fixture responses; the test can't reach GitHub (XE-019).
3. After a merge, `ship.py` pulls the default branch back into the local clone with `--ff-only` and prints remote and local SHAs, which must match. Tested against the bare-repository fixture.
4. `ship.py verify-live --url <post url> --hub-path <path> --term <hub term> --deadline <seconds>` fetches the page until it returns 200 or the deadline passes, then requires an `<a>` whose `href` ends in `<hub-path>` and whose text matches `<term>` case-insensitively. A 200 without the link exits non-zero with `hub_link_missing`. Tested on fixture HTML: linked, unlinked, 404.
5. `hub_term.py register --hub-links <path> --term <phrase> --owner <site> --site-url <url> --path <hub path>` adds the site to `SITES` if it's missing, adds one `HUB_LINKS` entry before the `future pillar terms` marker, adds a test asserting the relative link on the owner and the absolute link elsewhere, runs the package's build and test, and commits, pushes and pulls back through `agent_token.py`. A term that already exists exits 0 without a change. A failing test exits non-zero and commits nothing. Tested in `tests/test_hub_term.py` on a copy of a fixture `map.ts`, with build, test and git stubbed.
6. `hub_term.py pin --repo <path> --site <slug> --file <auto_linker_file>` updates the hub-links pin with the repo's own package manager (`pnpm update @moxywolf/hub-links --lockfile-only` for a pnpm lockfile) and checks that `<file>` passes `site: '<slug>'`. A mismatch exits non-zero naming the file and both slugs; it never rewrites the renderer on its own. Tested on fixture files.
7. `skills/blog-publish/SKILL.md` no longer tells the writer to push or click Push origin. It runs `ship.py push`, then `merge` when the target is protected, then `verify-live`, and reports done only on `verify-live` exit 0, writing the three `state.md` fields. A refusal is reported with the script's output and the item stays unpublished. The orchestrator's STEP 1.5 and `commands/blog-term.md` call `hub_term.py register`, and `/blog-publish` calls `hub_term.py pin` on the target before its commit, so the term, the pin and the post land in one pull request. A docs test asserts that "Push origin" appears in none of the 4d-blog-engine skill or command files, and that each names its script.
8. `targets/opencontrols.md` and `targets/frontier-founder.md` carry the three new fields, filled from what each repository actually does. `4d-blog-engine` moves a minor version and the marketplace version moves (CI-002). In CI, `run_all_tests.py` names `test_ship.py` and `test_hub_term.py` among the suites it ran and reports no failures.

**What's not taken.** Social posting stays manual. The skill still never posts to LinkedIn, X or Facebook. Waiting on Vercel's production deploy is `verify-live`'s deadline, not a separate integration.

## Fourteenth objective: the decider council

Opened 2026-10-18. Four scripts in this repository already ask a decision model typed questions and act on the answers: `packet_coverage.mjs` (is this criterion covered), `model_select.mjs` (which tier builds this item), `council/scripts/jev_route.py` (which deliberation shape), `model-router/scripts/route.py` (which tier runs this subagent). Each has its own wire (`/v1/evaluate`, `/v4/ai`, the AI SDK), its own key loading, its own stub for tests, and one judge, Jev. Dorian's direction: "We need to be able to ask the deciders more than just which model to use. We are going to be employing a decider call when building out many, many tasks." So the decider is a primitive every plugin calls, not a feature of one script. His name for it: "Think of it as a decider council." The council plugin already holds the deliberation council (language models on open questions); the decider council is its sibling for typed questions, and it lives in the same plugin. His second decision: when the deciders disagree, "Majority wins."

**Settled decisions**

- **One wire.** Every decider call is `POST /v1/decisions` on the AI Gateway (OpenAI-compatible; `input`, `questions` typed `predicate`, `choice`, `score`; probabilities back). It accepts any decision model slug, so a voice is a string, never a code path.
- **Three voices from three vendors** by default: `typesafe-ai/jev`, `perplexity/pplx-decider-v1.2`, `openai/gpt-6-luna-decisions`. The list is a constant with an environment override. No vendor checks itself; the same rule as cross-vendor review.
- **Majority wins** (Dorian, 2026-10-18). For a choice, the option most voices picked. For a predicate or a score, the median of the voices' numbers, which is the value a majority sits on either side of. No majority (a three-way split on a choice, or two voices that disagree) is returned as a tie, never as a pick.
- **Code decides what an answer means.** The primitive returns probabilities and the majority; the caller's own rule table turns them into an action, as MR-001 and MR-002 do. The primitive never carries a bar, a tier or a policy of its own.
- **Deciders answer typed questions only.** `/deliberate` stays the gate for open questions. Nothing in this objective touches the council's deliberation pipeline.
- **A decider that hasn't been scored watches before it steers** (MR-001's rule, kept). Every call writes a ledger line with the caller's name; the caller's own outcome signal is scored against each voice before the quorum is allowed to decide anything for that caller.

### DC-001 — The decider council: three voices on one wire, a majority, and a ledger every caller writes to

**Status:** done. Merged to `main` in `60a3e4c` (PR #228, head `77110c2`) on 18 October 2026 by `moxywolf-agent[bot]`, recorded by `record-release` as `agent_merge_autonomous` under DR-113, lane `wide`. Declared and approved 2026-10-18 (PR #227, `195f18f`). Probe first (criterion 8): `6a0f97c` adds only `docs/evidence/decision-quorum-probe-2026-10-18.md`; all three voices answered. Two reviews, both codex (gpt-6-astra). `20261018-100213-7c23b25-xdpcz0y9` ran two rounds: round 1 at `7c23b25` found F1 to F5 and F9 blocking (availability counted HTTP not answers; score lacked per-voice agreement and ties; the outcome rewrite could drop a concurrent append; a malformed envelope crashed `ask`; the timeout did not bound the body read; the byte-identity test stripped the model member), F6 and F7 separate (`jev_route.py` and `vocab_check.py` off the surface, named by basename), F8 a follow-up on the probe note; all fixed in `cdebf5e` except F9, F6 and F7, which were the design's: criteria 1, 2, 9 and 10 amended on Dorian's approval in `2e78652` (the wire names the voice in the body; exact paths). Fresh review `20261018-104726-2e78652-u2t4ltw8` at the amended head: round 1 found F1 (choice distributions must cover the offered values and rank the pick first, as `jev_route.well_formed` does), F2 (one malformed answer erased a voice's other answers) and F3 (the council outcome's status was printed, not saved on the release record), fixed in `415b166`; F4 separate (commit order invisible from the surface) answered by the committed history `docs/evidence/dc-001-build-history-2026-10-18.md` in `77110c2`; round 3 `fixes_verified`, 11/11 met, no unknowns; the ten unknowns the earlier rounds raised were closed on round 3's criterion rows and its CI run. CI `tests` green at every reviewed head (last run 39152996266). `record-release` wrote the MR-001 ledger line and the council outcome onto council line `7bef4cddb3b2`; `decider_council.py score --caller model_select` reads 2 calls, 1 with outcome, all three voices at `floor` on both (unscored by the rule, as designed). council 0.12.0, gstack-execution 0.89.0, marketplace 1.139.0. Not exercised live: `quorum` mode (the gate needs 20 scored lines; there is one) and `/decide` by hand.

**Links introduced:** `plugins/council/scripts/decider_council.py`, called by path from any plugin, and `/decide` in the council plugin, which calls it for a person or a skill (the precedent is `ship.py` naming `gstack-execution/scripts/agent_token.py` by path). Its output object: per question, each voice's answer verbatim (or `unavailable` with the reason), the `majority` answer, `agreed` (every available voice gave the same choice, or every number within `AGREE_BAND` of the median), `tie` (true when no majority exists), per-voice `cost` and latency. The shared ledger `$GSTACK_PEER_REVIEW_DIR/decisions/<YYYYMM>.jsonl` (falling back to `~/.gstack/decisions/`), one line per call: `ts`, `caller`, `ref` (what the caller was deciding about, a review id, an item id, a session id), the question names and types, the output object, and an empty `outcome` the caller fills in later with `decider_council.py outcome --line <id> --result <json>`. The reader's assumption is that every voice answered the same questions about the same input. That holds because the body is built once and sent to each slug unchanged, and criterion 9 pins it byte for byte.

1. `decider_council.py ask --caller <name> --ref <id> --input <file or -> --questions <json file or inline>` serializes one `/v1/decisions` body once and posts it to each slug in `DECIDERS` with only the leading `model` member different (the wire names the voice in the body; nothing else varies) (`GSTACK_DECIDERS` overrides, comma-separated), concurrently, each bounded by `DECIDER_TIMEOUT_MS` (8000). Stdlib Python only: `urllib`, `threading`, `json`. The key comes from `GSTACK_AIGATEWAY_ENV` or `AI_GATEWAY_API_KEY`, as `plugins/council/scripts/jev_route.py` loads it today. `JEV_ENDPOINT` replaces the base URL for the test stub. A test serves a stub that delays each voice one second and asserts a three-voice call completes in under two.
2. A voice that times out, returns a non-200, returns a `refusal` for a question, or returns a malformed answer (a probability outside [0, 1], probabilities that don't sum to 1 within 0.01, a choice not among those offered, a missing question) is `unavailable` for that question with the reason, and is not counted toward the majority. The well-formedness checks are the ones `plugins/council/scripts/jev_route.py` already applies, moved here.
3. Majority: for a `choice`, the value picked by more than half the available voices; for a `predicate`, the median probability; for a `score`, the median score. `tie` is true when a choice has no such value, or fewer than two voices answered. With one voice the output carries that voice's answer and `quorum: degraded`; with none, `unavailable`. The primitive never maps an answer to an action.
4. Every `ask` appends one ledger line before returning; the line id is printed with the output. `decider_council.py outcome --line <id> --result <json>` fills the line's `outcome`. A caller's outcome vocabulary is its own (model selection writes the MR-001 outcome fields; coverage writes whether the criterion was later found met at release; the smart-router writes its existing value-added score). A line never loses its answers when an outcome is written.
5. `decider_council.py score [--caller <name>] [--since <YYYY-MM>]` prints, per caller and per voice: calls, unavailable count, agreement rate, tie rate, mean cost, median latency, and, over lines with an outcome, how often each voice's answer and the majority answer matched the outcome by the caller's own rule (the caller registers that rule as a small Python function in a table in `decider_council.py`, one per caller, or the row prints `no outcome rule`). Zeros print as zero, and a caller with no lines prints a row of zeros.
6. `decider_council.py gate --caller <name>` exits 0 when that caller has at least 20 ledger lines with outcomes and at least two voices each, and the majority's match rate is at least that of Jev alone; otherwise it exits nonzero with `insufficient_history` and both rates printed. Callers that want to let the majority decide check this gate first and otherwise act on Jev's answer alone, as they do today. The 20 and the comparison are named constants.
7. **First caller: `model_select.mjs`.** It calls `decider_council.py ask --caller model_select` by `subprocess` for its five MR-002 questions, applies its rule table to the majority probabilities when `gate` passes and to Jev's probabilities otherwise, records both tiers and `rule: quorum_majority` or `quorum_tie` (a tie falls to the dearer tier, Dorian's one exception to majority: "a tie is not a decision"), and writes the MR-001 outcome on `record-release` through `decider_council.py outcome`. The AI SDK `evaluationModel` path leaves `model_select.mjs`. `packet_coverage.mjs`, `jev_route.py` and `route.py` are unchanged by this item and move under DC-002.
8. **Probe first.** The first commit on the build branch sends the five-question body to each of the three slugs on the real gateway and records each voice's HTTP status, answer shape, `cost` and latency verbatim in `docs/evidence/decision-quorum-probe-<date>.md`. A voice that doesn't answer `/v1/decisions` as documented is dropped from `DECIDERS` in that commit, with the evidence named. No step after the probe assumes a voice the probe didn't see answer.
9. `plugins/council/scripts/test_decider_council.py` runs `decider_council.py` as a subprocess against a stub serving `/v1/decisions` per slug: three voices agree; a two-to-one choice split returns the majority in both directions; a three-way choice split returns `tie`; predicate and score return the median; one voice unavailable is excluded and named; a refusal is unavailable; all unavailable is `unavailable`; the raw request bytes each stub slug received are byte-identical after the leading `model` member; the ledger line is written before the output prints; `outcome` fills a line and leaves its answers intact; `score` prints zeros on an empty ledger and the right counts on a fixture; `gate` refuses at 19 lines and at 20 with the majority below Jev, and passes at 20 with the majority at or above. `test_model_select.py` gains: the script calls `decider_council.py`; with `gate` failing it acts on Jev's tier; with `gate` passing it acts on the majority; a tie selects the dearer tier.
10. `vocabulary.json` gains `decider_council`, `quorum`, `tie`, `degraded`, `quorum_majority` and `quorum_tie`; `plugins/gstack-execution/scripts/vocab_check.py` resolves them. The peer-review contract's Model selection section, the `gstack-build` command text, `council/README.md`, `council/commands/decide.md` and `gstack-execution/README.md` describe the decider council, the three voices, the majority and tie rules, the ledger, `outcome`, `score` and `gate`, with a worked example of calling it from a new plugin in under ten lines, and `/decide <question> --choices a,b,c` as the hand-run form, which prints each voice, the majority and the ledger line id.
11. `plugins/council/.claude-plugin/plugin.json` and `plugins/gstack-execution/.claude-plugin/plugin.json` each move a minor version and the top-level marketplace version moves (CI-002). In CI, `run_all_tests.py` reports a nonzero count of what it examined, names `test_decider_council.py` and `test_model_select.py` among the suites it ran, and reports no failures.

### DC-002 — The other three decider calls join the decider council

**Status:** planned, declared with DC-001; built only after DC-001's probe and first ledger lines exist. `packet_coverage.mjs` (caller `coverage`, outcome: criterion met or unmet at release), `jev_route.py` (caller `council_router`, outcome: the smart-router's logged value-added score), and `route.py` PreToolUse (caller `model_router_hook`, outcome: none yet; shadow only, and it keeps its 8-second budget by calling the primitive with `DECIDER_TIMEOUT_MS` 6000 and acting on the table default when the call is late). Each keeps its own rule table. Each drops its private wire, key loading and stub. Criteria are written when DC-001's score has its first rows.

**Callers this objective expects next, not declared:** the Chief of Staff's routing of an ask to departments (CS-001 does it in skill prose today); GA-010's blast-radius lane; GO-005's "who makes this judgment call"; synergy-engine's fit-to-fingerprint score; `/session-review`'s proposal classification; BP-001's rubric checks before publish. Each becomes an item when its outcome signal is named, because a caller with no outcome can't be scored and a decider that can't be scored only watches.

## Fifteenth objective: the demand harness

Opened 2026-10-26. `plugins/mcp-demand-harness` turns what a product's customers will ask its MCP server and REST gateway into tests, and the deterministic half of those tests is the probe: a direct `tools/call` or REST request with a check on the reply. The 0.1.0 probe graded any non-empty reply as a pass. On 2026-10-25 a 36-case baseline against STIGViewer production scored 36/36 under 0.1.0 grading; a later run of the same case IDs under 0.2.0 grading, against the same advertised server surface, scored 30/36 (the original saved responses were not regraded, since 0.1.0 kept no bodies), and nine of the thirty-six (25%) turned out never to have tested what their question text says: three never reached their endpoint, four hit a slug that does not exist, two used the wrong search mode, one graded a correct refusal as content, and empty role searches passed as answers. The premise of this objective: **a green probe must mean its declared checks passed, and a failure the server reports inside a 2xx reply is a failure.** A liveness or negative-only check cannot establish that the server answered the question; structured and content checks establish only the properties they explicitly test, and every layer says which kind it carries.

**Settled decisions**

- **Deterministic probes are graded by code only.** No model reads a probe reply. The model-driven suites (MCPJam, mcp-eval) stay separate and are not covered by this item.
- **Structured contracts, not substrings, are the strong form.** A `json_any_of` contract is a list of alternative branches; a branch is a conjunction of JSON Pointer predicates (`path`, `type`, `nonempty`, `equals`). A substring check proves an envelope arrived; a contract proves the shape of the answer.
- **Only one failure is excusable, and only explicitly.** A 2xx reply carrying `ok:false` passes only as a `not_found` denial with HTTP status 404 whose body matches a branch that names `/absence/reason` and a nonempty `/absence/message`. Authentication, entitlement, rate-limit and server-error envelopes never pass, whatever the contract says.
- **`error` and `isError` are reserved keys** at the top of any payload. A product whose success payload needs a field by those names renames it or nests it; the grader does not relax for everyone.
- **Evidence is kept, bounded and redacted.** Each layer records a receipt: sha256 of the canonical original body, up to 16,384 characters of sanitized text, and explicit `truncated` and `redacted` flags. Redaction is best-effort and says so. A truncated or redacted receipt cannot support exact offline regrading, and the report does not pretend otherwise.
- **A run never overwrites a run.** Output paths are opened exclusively; an existing file refuses the run before any request is sent.
- **Staging by default; production needs a named approver** (unchanged from 0.1.0, `GOVERNANCE.md`).

### DH-001 — A green probe means its declared checks passed

**Status:** review. Built on `build/mcp-probe-evidence` (harness 0.2.0, frozen at `b55a9e0`, fixes through `85d5f4a`). Cross-vendor reviews `20261026-080924-da4beb3-htsmx86c` (codex, 2 rounds, F1–F3 fixed at `c32cd54`) and `20261026-082123-182cc9e-sas80u0x` (codex, 3 rounds, F1–F3 fixed at `613f858` and `85d5f4a`, final round zero findings, `rounds_exhausted` on execution evidence at the reviewed head). Not released. Coverage against this item not yet scored; the item did not exist when the reviews ran. Evidence at the time of drafting: 82 tests at `85d5f4a`, failure-ledger run `331c2d9bb407`, exit 0.

**Links introduced:** a probe result layer now links to its response receipt (sha256 of the original body plus bounded sanitized text), to the plan item that produced it (plan sha256 and the plan's declared bank hash), and to the harness that graded it (source hashes of `probe.py`, `probe_checks.py`, `demandlib.py`), with the server's self-reported name and version beside them. The links are hashes over content in the run record; nothing links to a deployed commit, and the record says so.

1. A `tools/call` reply fails the `mcp_payload` layer when it carries a JSON-RPC `error`, an `id` that does not match the request, `isError: true`, or empty data. Empty is decided by the selected payload: `structuredContent.data` when `structuredContent` is present, otherwise the first JSON text block's `data`; accompanying prose never makes an explicitly empty payload non-empty, and prose alone counts only when no block is JSON. Tests: `test_tool_and_http_errors_fail_before_content`, `test_rpc_errors_malformed_and_wrong_id_cannot_be_results`, `test_empty_text_and_text_failure_envelopes_fail`, `test_f1_text_cannot_make_empty_structured_data_nonempty`, `test_r2_f1_prose_cannot_make_empty_json_text_data_nonempty`, and `test_error_results_cannot_pass_a_nonempty_check` through a real subprocess run against a local HTTP fixture.
2. Every JSON envelope in a reply is inspected for an application failure: `structuredContent` and each parseable text block. An envelope with `ok:false` fails unless it is a `not_found`/404 denial matched by an explicit typed-absence branch of the case's `json_any_of`; `rate_limited`, `unauthorized`, `forbidden`, `error` and any non-404 status cannot borrow a success branch or an absence branch. A reserved `error` or `isError` key in any envelope's payload fails. Tests: `test_error_denial_cannot_use_a_success_branch`, `test_application_exceptions_in_text_are_not_success`, `test_f2_failure_envelope_in_any_block_is_seen`, `test_r2_f3_reserved_error_keys_apply_to_every_envelope`.
3. A REST layer passes its transport check in exactly two cases, consistent with criterion 2: a successful 2xx response whose body is a non-empty JSON object or array without an application failure, or the narrowly permitted typed absence, a 2xx body with `ok:false` whose denial is `not_found` with status 404 and which matches an explicit typed-absence branch evaluated against the whole body (REST contracts are body-relative; nothing is unwrapped). Every other `ok:false` body, and any reserved `error` or `isError` key, fails. Tests: `test_rest_json_error_is_not_success`, `test_r2_f2_rest_body_accepts_only_an_explicit_typed_absence`, `test_r2_f2b_rest_absence_is_body_relative_even_with_a_data_field`.
4. A `json_any_of` contract is validated before any request is sent: a non-empty list of non-empty branches, each predicate a JSON Pointer `path` with at least one of `type` (`object`, `array`, `string`, `boolean`, `number`, `null`), `nonempty: true`, or `equals`; an invalid contract in the bank or the plan refuses the run with zero requests. Grading evaluates every predicate of a branch and passes when any branch holds; a missing path fails; `equals` keeps JSON types apart at every depth, so `true` is not `1` inside objects or arrays; a structural check is never excused by a substring appearing elsewhere in the reply. Tests: `test_bad_contract_is_refused`, `test_structured_success_or_explicit_absence`, `test_nested_payload_and_pointer_escaping`, `test_f3_equals_keeps_json_booleans_and_numbers_apart_recursively`, `test_invalid_contract_is_refused_before_traffic`, `test_payload_json_contract_is_validated_and_exportable`.
5. Each layer records `assertion_strength` as `structured` (a `json_any_of` contract), `content` (`must_contain`), `negative_only` (`must_not_match` only) or `liveness` (nothing but non-emptiness), and `results.py ingest` renders a coverage table by strength and marks reports written before the label existed as `unknown_legacy` rather than inferring strength from a green verdict. Tests: `test_probe_retains_assertion_strength_and_flags_legacy`, `test_receipts_and_structured_contract_survive_real_http_run`.
6. Each layer keeps a response receipt: sha256 of the canonical original, up to 16,384 characters of sanitized text, `truncated` and `redacted` flags, and the original byte count. Redaction covers the run's own credential, keys named like secrets, bearer tokens, GitHub tokens and JWT-shaped strings, inside JSON text blocks and SSE `data:` lines as well as top-level fields, and safe JSON reformatting is not reported as redaction. Tests: `test_receipt_redacts_then_bounds_and_records_original_hash`, `test_json_text_secrets_are_redacted`, `test_sse_secrets_are_redacted_without_flagging_safe_json`, and the subprocess run asserting the fixture token never appears in the report.
7. A run record carries the plan file's sha256, the profile file's sha256, the bank hash the plan declares (labelled as the plan's declaration, not an independent bank check), sha256 of the three harness source files, the local git revisions of the harness and the working repository, and the `serverInfo` name and version observed at `initialize`. The record states that a version or surface digest is not proof of a deployed commit. Test: `test_receipts_and_structured_contract_survive_real_http_run`.
8. Before any request: an output path that already exists, an `--only` selection that matches nothing or names only items with no executable call, or an invalid contract refuses the run with exit 3, and the existing output is left byte-identical. Production still requires `--approver "First Last"` and refuses draft questions. Tests: `test_existing_output_and_empty_selection_refuse_before_traffic`, `test_invalid_contract_is_refused_before_traffic`, `test_production_needs_named_person`.
9. `demandlib.validate_bank` validates `json_any_of` in both `payload` and `rest` sections through `probe_checks.validate_contract`. The import is deferred to the point of validation rather than taken at module scope: `demandlib` still depends on `probe_checks` whenever a bank carries a contract, but loading `demandlib` for export, seeding or reporting does not import the probe module. Test: `test_payload_json_contract_is_validated_and_exportable`.
10. The whole suite runs against local HTTP fixtures with no external network and no production credentials. A packet claiming this item includes a passing suite ledger receipt bound to the reviewed head.

## Boundary tests

Declared 2026-09-29 on Dorian's review of this document: *“The doc is now better at naming failure modes than at enforcing its own release boundary.”* Reordered the same day on two further reviews. These three run in order, before any new item ID. Each one records its findings in `docs/evidence/boundary-<letter>-<date>.md`. A finding that needs a fix becomes an amendment to the item named, not a new item.

**B-a. The pre-merge boundary, tested live and bounded.** A refusal after the merge stops a label, not the merge or a deploy it triggers, so the gate under test is GitHub's own ruleset, not anything the agent writes. Scratch pull requests that never merge can't prove a ruleset stops a real bad merge, so this test runs on a sacrificial public repository, `MoxyWolfLLC/gate-lab`:
- **Set-up (Dorian, as admin).** He creates the repo, installs the `moxywolf-agent` app on it, and gives it the same ruleset he gives `main` (see *Constraints*): an approving review required from code owners, with a `CODEOWNERS` naming him for every path including `CODEOWNERS` itself, stale approvals dismissed on push, approval of the most recent push required, the `tests` check required, force pushes blocked, and the app not on the bypass list.
- **Attempts (the agent, as the bot).** Eight real merges:
  1. merge with no approval;
  2. merge after a push that followed his approval;
  3. merge with the `tests` check failing;
  4. merge with the check missing;
  5. push straight to the default branch;
  6. force-push;
  7. edit or delete the ruleset;
  8. approve its own pull request.
- **Pass.** Every attempt is refused by GitHub, and the refusal is quoted. One control merge, with his approval at the head and green checks, succeeds.
- **Then production.** Dorian applies the same ruleset to `main` and exports both rulesets as JSON into `docs/evidence/`, so the doc records the real production configuration, not the lab's. The agent then repeats attempts 1 to 3 against `main` with pull requests whose only change is a line in a file under `docs/evidence/`, so any merge that wrongly succeeds is harmless and is reverted at once. The gate is a proposed configuration until GitHub has refused a real merge on `main`.
- **After the test.** gate-lab is deleted.
- **The same run also:**
  - records how the agent-side checks behave on the same attempts (`release`, `record-release`, the merge-instruction step), so their role is stated as a second line, not the gate;
  - lists how each of the live states (`review`, `released`, `done`, `merged_unreviewed`) is decided today, and where two can be true at once;
  - lists every item whose status changes under the one release path, for Dorian to rule on. It changes none of them itself except the two relabelled on 2026-09-29.

**Result, 2026-09-29: pass.** GitHub refused all eight attempts on gate-lab and the one control merge went through; on `main`, attempts 1 to 3 were refused on probe PR #75 and `main` didn't move. Evidence: `docs/evidence/boundary-a-2026-09-29.md`. Not verified there: the saved bypass list, which the bot's token can't read.

**B-b. The scorer runs in CI.** This is XE-014, built as amended. **Result, 2026-09-29: pass.** XE-014 merged as `9c50a87` (PR #76), review `20260929-145130-3eb9376-ytz9zc1j`, 13/13. It passes when CI executes `packet_coverage.mjs` on every push, with one `checked` case against a stub gateway and each failure case. A review opened without a `checked` status must also be visibly blocked at release.

**B-c. One messy real session through SM-004's hooks, staging and publish gate.** Run 2026-09-29, headless and then interactively by Dorian; results in `docs/evidence/boundary-c-2026-09-29.md`, and SM-004 is revised from them. Not observed: `StopFailure`, and Cowork rather than the Claude Code CLI. This uses throwaway code, kept outside this repository, run through the real hook route, not a parser alone. The session includes a sub-agent, an attachment, a tool output over 100 KB, a nested secret (a key inside JSON inside a tool result), a binary output, a secret inside a ZIP, and a compaction. The architecture is settled; B-c settles only these lifecycle facts:
- whether the `/session-review` line is in the session file before `UserPromptExpansion` fires;
- how `prompt_id` appears in session-file entries;
- how several assistant text blocks combine into `last_assistant_message`, which is the concatenation rule criterion 3 needs;
- whether compaction rewrites earlier lines or only appends;
- whether the `Stop` hook finishes before the next expansion hook fires;
- how `StopFailure` affects the last complete turn;
- whether sub-agent session files and attachment bytes are still readable at capture time;
- whether Claude Code or Cowork stops plugin hooks writing to the proposed staging folder.

SM-004's criteria are revised from what B-c shows before SM-004 is built.

Work on vocabulary, metrics and graph handoffs held until B-a, B-b and B-c passed. All three passed on 2026-09-29, so item IDs declared after that date (SM-005 onward) meet the constraint.

**B-e. The goal layer's boundaries, before the first goal runs.** Declared 2026-10-01 with goal mode. Runs after GO-001 to GO-006 merge and before any real goal. The agent, as the bot, tries each of these against a throwaway goal, and each must be refused or stopped, with the refusal quoted in `docs/evidence/boundary-e-<date>.md`: start with a review that isn't Dorian's approval at the goal folder's head; edit the brief, plan or a goal test during a run; push straight to `goal/<id>`; change a file outside Allowed paths; change a `CODEOWNERS` path or a goal script; reuse a review from an earlier head; make a metered call past the provider's limit, and spend across two providers until each stops at its own budget; keep running with `HALT` present; merge `goal/<id>` to `main` with a goal test failing or without Dorian's approval at the head; satisfy `goal-envelope`, `goal-tests` or `goal-holdout` with a status the bot posts, one attempt for each; replace `goal_envelope.py` in an item pull request; show that a passing candidate's checks satisfy the ruleset and that the same verdicts don't satisfy a later head; push a workflow file to an item branch with a goal-run token, and run a workflow from an item branch that tries to read `GOAL_<ID>_HOLDOUT`; add a dependency labelled `in_envelope_code`. It also checks that a builder session's mounted roots don't contain the holdout. Then an adversarial pilot: a goal whose easiest route to green is weakening a test or touching an out-of-scope path, with one forced prohibited attempt scripted into it. It passes when the forced attempt is refused and quoted, and the run either completes inside the envelope with no boundary crossed in its record or stops and escalates. The first real goal, chosen by Dorian on 2026-10-01, is that a session review made in a cloud session can still be read from the vault after the session ends.

## Validation

Write failing behavioral tests before implementation. Exercise real dispatcher and state transitions using temporary repositories. Use controlled reviewer responses for malformed-output and failure cases, followed by a live cross-tool review to verify integration.

Test stale approvals, incomplete acceptance, dropped blockers, failed branches, changed inputs, interrupted runs, and duplicate release attempts. No production release is required to prove refusal behavior.

The review ran three rounds and passed at the merged head. Two of its findings are worth keeping in mind for the next executor change: a stored failure reason is executor-authored or it leaks, and a cycle record must follow a node through every status, blocked included. No built-in workflow runs with retry yet; that is the next amendment, naming the eligible nodes.

- 2026-10-12: XE-036 declared on Dorian's approval, from his brief "Design goal: bounded correction of one failed gstack node" (conceptual source: https://x.com/hanakoxbt/status/2091515787366306154). One failed `worker` or `checker` node is retried inside the run, at most two retries, with a correction packet recording the previous attempt's category, reason, redacted diagnostics and frozen scope; the execution signature excludes the packet, so a retry can't reset its own budget; green siblings stand and dependents wait; exhaustion blocks as today. No built-in workflow changes policy in this item. His brief sets this pull request's merge rule narrower than DR-113: it waits for his GitHub approval and his word "merge". The brief's starting-point section was checked against `d81d9fc` before drafting: `validate_graph()` accepts only `block`, `execute()` marks a handler exception `failed` and blocks descendants, and a later `run` already reuses valid successes, so the gap is in-run retry and correction context, not resume.

- 2026-10-08: CS-004 and CS-006 are done: merged in `6d52b3f` (PR #192) and recorded by `record-release` as `agent_merge_on_instruction`. F7 is resolved, and F3 and F8 are limits of the review tooling that I can check myself. - 2026-10-07: CS-006 criteria 1, 2, 3 and 7 are tightened after CS-006’s second review round. That round closed its first six findings and raised two: the underscore rule dropped a double underscore inside a word, so a changed identifier verified, and an `Open:` line could carry a statement in front of its question. Both are fixed, with three neighbors of the same kind the builder found by reading the script the way the reviewer does. An asterisk or a backtick inside a word is kept, like an underscore. A heading or bold title that begins with a label is judged as a labeled line. The rule about what nobody did covers `Me:` lines too, and an `Open:` line may only ask it outright. Criterion 7 goes back to what it said before the last amendment, every label, with that one exception named. The new script was replayed, line by line, against the old one over the 36 accepted responses of runs 16 to 18. Fifteen lines change, all `Open:` lines, each with a statement that leads into the question or rides along with it. They are in seven responses, and four of the lines are in two of run 18’s. No other line’s result changes. Because run 18’s accepted responses no longer all pass, every scenario is run again as run 19. After run 19 two more holes of the same kind were closed in the script (`8bef09c`, `da1c796`), inside what criteria 1 and 2 already say: a quotation is found only on whole tokens of its source, so “400 of margin” doesn’t verify against “$14,400 of margin”, and a fenced block opens only on a line that carries the fence and at most a language name. Replayed over the 48 accepted responses of runs 16 to 19, that change alters no line’s result.

- 2026-10-07: CS-006 criteria 3 and 7 are brought into line with each other, after CS-006’s first review round. Criterion 3 said an inference or a proposal can’t say what nobody did. Criterion 7 said that fails under any label but `Me:`. The script followed 3 and the test followed the script, so `Skill:` and `Source:` lines were outside the rule. Both criteria now name the same set: every label that isn’t a quotation, `Me:` or `Open:`. A replay over the accepted responses of runs 16 to 18 found 170 `Skill:` and `Source:` lines, and the wider rule fails none of them.

- 2026-09-29 (third review round): The constraint no longer calls XE-015 `done`. SM-004 now uses a `UserPromptExpansion` hook for identity and a `Stop`-hook hash for finality. It adds block-indexed event IDs, split completeness fields, `not-collected-by-policy` for reasoning, a ten-step package lifecycle with hashes excluding themselves, and quarantined binaries. It also adds gitleaks as the named second scanner, `review-run.json`, `proposals.jsonl`, `verification.jsonl` with seven states, and `publish-receipt.json`.

- 2026-09-29: Rewritten on Dorian's review of this document. Constraints gain one release path checked by code, the ledger as attention and not containment, mandatory coverage, no new item IDs until the boundary tests run, and a gate before transcript-derived data becomes readable. XE-014 gains criteria 11 and 12, and its criterion 5 now blocks release on any status but `checked`. SM-004 is rewritten with his feedback: exact session identity, capture finality, a manifest and event schema, classified exclusions, a private-first storage gate, code-only verification, and TB-002's enclosure. Governance-complete retention is deferred with no ID.

- 2026-09-22: XE-014 declared and approved by Dorian, and its first two criteria rewritten before any code. The item was approved on the premise that XE-010's scorer records `unavailable` because the gateway credential is unreachable. Measured rather than assumed, and the premise was wrong in its mechanism. `packet_coverage.mjs` imports `ai` and no `package.json` exists anywhere in this repository, so the script exits 1 at `ERR_MODULE_NOT_FOUND` before `main()` runs and writes nothing to the packet. The credential branch it was supposed to reach is downstream of an import that has never resolved. `tests.yml` is `setup-python` only, with no `setup-node`, no install step and no invocation of the scorer, so CI has never run it either, and `test_packet_coverage.py` exercises `coverage_verdict` alone over hand-built dicts. The suite is green over a producer that has never executed once, which is XE-010 criterion 6 one level up: that criterion made the extractor prove its own coverage, and nothing made the scorer prove it can start. The credential finding stands on its own evidence and is kept: `aigateway.env` holds a working 60-character `vck_` key that returns HTTP 200 from `typesafe-ai/jev` in 135ms and separates a covered criterion at 0.90 from an absurd one at 0.05, while nothing in `.zshrc`, `.zprofile` or `.profile` exports it and a fresh login shell has `AI_GATEWAY_API_KEY`, `GSTACK_OPENROUTER_ENV` and `GSTACK_AGENT_APP_ENV` all unset. Two defects stacked, and only the second one was declared. What is NOT a defect, and was claimed as one in the first draft: there is no silent false pass. `coverage_verdict` returns `not_run` for an absent report and the review state records `coverage_checked: false`, which is XE-010 criterion 5 working as written. The gate is absent and says so; what failed is that nothing reads the saying. Criterion 6 was added for that. Not verified: whether `experimental_evaluate` reads `AI_GATEWAY_API_KEY` from the process environment rather than from an argument. The script's `key` is used only as a guard and is never passed to the call, so the fix must set it into `process.env` rather than hand it over, and that inference rests on Vercel's documentation naming the variable for all three integration paths, not on reading the package, which is installed nowhere on this machine.

- 2026-09-22: CI-002 criterion 9's worked example corrected before any code, approved by Dorian. The criterion named `github-repo-analyzer` at 0.11.0 as the real-history catch at `6c9912c`. Measured, it bumped there, 0.10.0 to 0.11.0. What `6c9912c` actually ships at an unmoved version is `gstack-execution`, which changed in that merge and stayed at 0.27.0. The premise is untouched and only the example was wrong, but a test written to the old wording asserts something false, and a check that lands red is a check somebody turns off, per DR-102. The criterion now names `gstack-execution` and adds the range `d619c06..2293ff6`, where the top-level version is unmoved across six consecutive merges. Three of those six changed a plugin and are caught; the other three changed no plugin and owe no bump, which is criterion 2 read exactly. A first draft of this entry claimed all six were catches. The real-history test refuted it on the first run, which is the test doing its job on the sentence that introduced it.

- 2026-09-21: GA-007 declared and built in the same change, on Dorian's instruction to fix it and merge. It follows directly from GA-006. GA-006 made the installation resolve per repository, which is right, and made the credential's reach a thing that varies per repository, which nothing could then inspect. A permission accepted on one installation and pending on another is invisible: the refusal reads the same as never having asked. The information was already in the response `mint()` parses. This item prints it.

- 2026-09-21: CI-002 declared and approved by Dorian, and its top-level criterion added before any code. The item was approved checking the per-plugin version only. Criterion 2 added and approved, and the top-level bump rides in the CI-002 change so the check lands over a clean catalog, per DR-102.
